L1633: Procedures performed as part of subject care within the 42-day period before the first dose of study drug may have been used for screening purposes if they conformed to protocol requirements and standards. All screening test results were reviewed prior to dosing to assess the study candidate's eligibility for inclusion.

The Part 1 Completion Visit applied to subjects who were not continuing into Part 2. Those continuing into Part 2 instead had the assessments indicated at P2-C1D1 (seeSubjects who exited the study with radiologic disease progression underwent their last study evaluation 28 days ± 7 days after their last dose of study drug or before any new PVNS therapy, including surgery, whichever occurred first. Any planned new PVNS therapy, including the type of surgical procedure, was recorded. Subjects who withdrew from the study with radiologic progression did not undergo an End-of-Study/Early Termination MRI.Subjects who ended their study participation with no radiologic disease progression underwent post-treatment procedures 28 days ± 7 days after their last dose of study drug and a final MRI 12 weeks ± 7 days after their last dose of study drug or before any new PVNS therapy, including surgery, whichever occurred first. The latter MRI did not need to be performed if new PVNS therapy started within 4 weeks of the Post-treatment Visit. Before or at the End-of-Study/Early Termination Visit, plans for any new PVNS therapy were obtained (see Footnote z).Weight only.Vital signs were performed before any invasive procedures were carried out on the same day.In the event of early withdrawal or if the subject completed Part 1 but did not enter Part 2, a physical examination was performed at the Post-treatment Visit.A standard 12-lead ECG was performed prior to dosing. Subjects were told not to take their morning dose of study drug at home on days when an ECG was performed; instead, they brought their study drug bottle to the study site and took their morning dose upon study site instruction. At the P1-C1D1 and P1-C1D15 Visits only, the ECG was performed before and 2 hours after dosing. The 2-hour postdose ECG began within 10 minutes of the 2-hour postdose time point.ECHO or MUGA scanning was chosen by investigator preference. However, the same imaging platform must have been used throughout the study.ECHO or MUGA scanning was only repeated if more than 3 months had elapsed since the last procedure. Subjects who showed a reduced ejection fraction relative to Baseline at the last visit were followed until a stable ejection fraction was measured by 2 consecutive testsALP, ALT, AST, and total and direct bilirubin were assessed at screening, weekly during P1-C1 beginning on Day 1, on Days 1 and 15 of P1-C2, and on Day 1 of every cycle thereafterSubjects receiving concomitant warfarin had their anticoagulation status carefully monitored for any necessary dose adjustments (see Section 6.4.8).Women who were not using hormonal contraception were tested for levels of FSH, LH, progesterone, and estradiol. Hormone testing was not required for women who had either an oophorectomy or were postmenopausal. Men were tested for levels of LH, FSH, and testosterone. Men whose testosterone level was below Baseline at the last study visit were followed until their level had stabilized or returned to Baseline.Subjects recorded responses to the BPI Worst Pain NRS item, Worst Stiffness NRS item, PROMIS Physical Function Scale, and EQ-5D-5L in the eDiary before any invasive procedures (NOTE: at the Screening Visit only, the recall period was the preceding week for the BPI Worst Pain NRS item and Worst Stiffness NRS item. The subject's response was documented in the medical notes).Subjects were reminded to complete the BPI Worst Pain NRS item and the Worst Stiffness NRS item, at least 4 of 7 consecutive days for 2 weeks prior to randomization, and prior to Day 1 of P1-C1, P1-C3, and P1-C5 and before the Part 1 Completion Visit (Week 25). In the event of early withdrawal or if the subject completed Part 1 but did not enter Part 2, this item was completed prior to the Post-treatment Visit.The investigator may have requested a centrally read blinded MRI to confirm progression, eg. if a subject's mid-study clinical profile or local radiological assessment indicated A ROM assessment in the affected joint or tumor location was performed by a blinded independent third-party assessor.Women of childbearing potential had a serum pregnancy test within 14 day's prior to randomization (or, where different regulations apply, within 72 hours of randomization) (Women who had documentation of at least 12 months of spontaneous amenorrhea and had an FSH level &gt;40 mIU/mL were assessed as postmenopausal and did not need to undergo pregnancy testing).Archival surgical tissue from TGCT tumors was obtained at Screening (or whenever available) for biomarker assessment.Details on blood sampling are found in Section 6.5.2.1 (PK) and Section 6.5.2.2 (PDy).  A diary of analgesic use was kept for the 2-week period before the first dose of study drug.The moming dose of study drug was administered at the study site on the days indicated; P1-C1D1 was within 3 days after randomization.

Dose administration by the subject at home. See Section 6.1 for dose reduction as of P1-C1D15. Subjects took their last dose of study drug the evening before their scheduled Part 1 Completion Visit (Week 25).After the subject provided signed informed consent; AEs were monitored throughout the study via safety assessments, observation, and subject reporting (see Section 6.5.3).If surgical resection of the tumor was performed within 12 weeks after the last dose of study drug, details of the surgery and its outcome were obtained.

L1729: Diagram illustrating the ENLIVEN Study Schema.
Part 1 (Randomized, placebo-controlled, & blinded):
Pexidartinib 500 mg BID for 2 wks, then 400 mg BID
Placebo (matching)
Part 2 (Open-label extension and cross-over):
Pexidartinib current dose or 400 mg BID
Eligibility: Patients with PVNS or GCT-TS. Planned: 126 patients with 1:1 randomization stratified by:
Region (US vs. non-US countries)
Disease location (upper vs. lower extremity involvement)
Continuation: Continue until PD or unacceptable toxicity, withdrawal of consent, or death.

Figure 14 displays two Kaplan-Meier survival curves for Pexidartinib-Treated Rats. The left graph shows survival for Males, and the right graph shows survival for Females. Both graphs plot Kaplan-Meier Estimate (%) on the Y-axis against Study Day on the X-axis. The curves show survival rates for four groups: Vehicle, DI Water, 1 mg/kg/day, 3 mg/kg/day, and 10 mg/kg/day. In both graphs, the survival rates for the treated groups (1, 3, and 10 mg/kg/day) are generally lower than the control groups (Vehicle and DI Water), indicating a dose-dependent decrease in survival. The curves for the treated groups appear to converge towards the control group survival rate after approximately 500 Study Days.
L1736: Figure 15 displays four Goodness-of-Fit (GoF) plots for the final model, arranged in a 2x2 grid. The plots compare observed data against population and individual predictions, and residuals.
Top Left Plot: Observation (ng/mL) vs Population Prediction (ng/mL). The scatter plot shows observations (Y-axis, 0 to 20000) versus population predictions (X-axis, 0 to 20000). A diagonal line (y=x) and a fitted curve (red line) are overlaid, indicating a strong fit.
Top Right Plot: Observation (ng/mL) vs Individual Prediction (ng/mL). The scatter plot shows observations (Y-axis, 0 to 20000) versus individual predictions (X-axis, 0 to 20000). A diagonal line (y=x) and a fitted curve (red line) are overlaid, indicating a strong fit.
Bottom Left Plot: CWRESI (Weighted Residuals) vs Population Prediction (ng/mL). The scatter plot shows CWRESI (Y-axis, -5 to 10) versus population predictions (X-axis, 0 to 20000). The residuals are centered around zero, showing a slight negative trend, which is acceptable.
Bottom Right Plot: CWRESI (Weighted Residuals) vs Time after last dose (Hrs). The scatter plot shows CWRESI (Y-axis, -5 to 10) versus time after last dose (X-axis, 0 to 400). The residuals are centered around zero, showing a slight negative trend, which is acceptable.

L1741: Figure 20 displays three Kaplan-Meier (KM) curves showing the Event Probability (Y-axis) versus Time (Months) (X-axis) for patients with Mild hepatic impairment (N=28, red line) and Normal liver function (N=184, blue line).
The top left plot is titled "AST 3 fold ULN". The top right plot is titled "ALT 3 fold ULN". The bottom plot is titled "TBIL 2 fold Baseline".
In all three plots, the Mild group consistently shows a higher event probability (faster onset and higher incidence) compared to the Normal group.
L1742: Figure 21 displays four panels (A, B, C, D) comparing PBPK predicted mean plasma concentration-time profiles (solid black lines) and observed individual plasma concentration-time profiles (circles) for pexidartinib.
Panel A: Systemic Concentration (ng/mL) vs Time (h) for a single oral dose of 1800 mg. The Y-axis ranges from 0 to 18000 ng/mL. The predicted profile shows rapid absorption and elimination.
Panel B: Log-transformed Systemic Concentration (ng/mL) vs Time (h) for a single oral dose of 1800 mg. The Y-axis is logarithmic, ranging from 1 to 100000 ng/mL. The predicted profile shows rapid absorption and elimination.
Panel C: Systemic Concentration (ng/mL) vs Time (h) for the last dosing interval following a 1000 mg daily dose (split 400 mg: 600 mg) for 14 days. The Y-axis ranges from 0 to 25000 ng/mL. The predicted profile shows a steady-state concentration profile.
Panel D: Log-transformed Systemic Concentration (ng/mL) vs Time (h) for the last dosing interval following a 1000 mg daily dose (split 400 mg: 600 mg) for 14 days. The Y-axis is logarithmic, ranging from 100 to 100000 ng/mL. The predicted profile shows a steady-state concentration profile.
The grey dashed lines in Panels A and B represent the 5% and 95% percentiles of the observed data.
L1743: Figure 22 displays two 3D surface plots showing the Sensitivity Analysis (SA) of the CYP2C8 and OATP1B1 K_i values of Pexidartinib on the Cmax Ratio (left) and AUC Ratio (right) for repaglinide.
Left Plot: Cmax Ratio (repaglinide)
X-axis: CYP2C8 K_i (ranging from 0.25 to 2.40).
Y-axis: SLCO1B1 (OATP1B1) K_i Liver (ranging from 0.25 to 5.16).
Z-axis: Cmax Ratio (ranging from 0.980 to 1.060).
Right Plot: AUC Ratio (repaglinide)
X-axis: CYP2C8 K_i (ranging from 0.25 to 2.40).
Y-axis: SLCO1B1 (OATP1B1) K_i Liver (ranging from 0.25 to 5.16).
Z-axis: AUC Ratio (ranging from 0.960 to 1.100).
The plots illustrate that the predicted Cmax and AUC ratios for repaglinide are relatively stable across the tested range of CYP2C8 and OATP1B1 K_i values, indicating minimal drug interaction effect.
L1744: Figure 23 displays two 2D line graphs showing the Sensitivity Analysis (SA) of the CYP2C8 K_i values of Pexidartinib on the Cmax Ratio (left) and AUC Ratio (right) for rosiglitazone.
Left Plot: Cmax Ratio (Rosiglitazone)
X-axis: CYP2C8 K_i (ranging from 0.00 to 4.00).
Y-axis: Cmax Ratio (ranging from 1.000 to 1.007).
Right Plot: AUC Ratio (Rosiglitazone)
X-axis: CYP2C8 K_i (ranging from 0.00 to 4.00).
Y-axis: AUC Ratio (ranging from 1.000 to 1.050).
Both plots show that the predicted Cmax Ratio and AUC Ratio for rosiglitazone decrease slightly as the CYP2C8 K_i value increases, indicating a minimal drug interaction effect.

L1748: Figure 28 is a spaghetti plot illustrating the Range of Motion (ROM) Change From Baseline (Raw Scores) over time (Baseline, Week 13 Visit, Week 25) for two treatment arms: Pexidartinib (red lines) and Placebo (teal lines). The Y-axis ranges from -100 to 100 Raw Scores. The X-axis shows Baseline, Week 13 Visit, and Week 25. The plot shows individual patient trajectories, indicating high variability in ROM change. At Week 13, Pexidartinib generally shows higher ROM scores compared to Placebo. By Week 25, the distributions overlap significantly, with both groups showing a wide range of ROM scores, mostly centered around 0 to 50.
L1749: Figure 29 is a waterfall plot showing the ROM Change From Baseline at Week 25 (Raw Scores) by Treatment Arm (Pexidartinib and Placebo). The Y-axis ranges from -100 to 100 Raw Scores. The X-axis represents the number of patients (0 to 75+). The plot shows the distribution of ROM change scores at Week 25. Pexidartinib (red bars) generally shows a positive change (improvement) in ROM, with scores clustered between 0 and 50. Placebo (teal bars) shows a negative change (worsening) in ROM, with scores clustered between -50 and 0. The waterfall plot visually demonstrates that Pexidartinib resulted in a greater improvement in ROM compared to Placebo at Week 25.
L1750: Figure 30 displays two scatter plots showing ROM Change From Baseline at Week 25 (Y-axis, ranging from -20 to 60) versus Percent Change Tumor Shrinkage (X-axis). The left plot uses RECIST v1.1, and the right plot uses Tumor Volume Score (TVS).
Both plots show data points categorized by Tumor Response (Non-Responder: black dots, Responder: red triangles) and Treatment (Pexidartinib: red circles, Placebo: black circles). Vertical dashed lines indicate thresholds for tumor shrinkage: \ge 30\% Decrease (left plot) and \ge 50\% Decrease (right plot).
The left plot (RECIST v1.1) shows a general trend of increasing ROM with increasing tumor shrinkage, particularly for Pexidartinib responders. The right plot (TVS) shows a similar trend, with responders generally having higher ROM values than non-responders, especially at higher shrinkage levels.
